## 1. Introduction

The atmos<sub>p</sub>here is chaotic (Lorenz 1963): small initial-state errors <sub>g</sub>row ex<sub>p</sub>onentiall<sub>y</sub>, so be<sub>y</sub>ond <sub>a</sub> f<sub>ew</sub> d<sub>ays</sub> <sub>a</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t <sub>mus</sub>t <sub>carry</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>f it<sub>s</sub> <sub>own</sub> <sub>uncer</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>y.</sub> O<sub>pera</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>cen</sub>t<sub>res</sub> <sub>answer</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith ensembles (Leith 1974; Buizza and Palmer 1995; Palmer 2019); the Euro<sub>p</sub>ean Centre for Medium-Ran<sub>g</sub>e Weather Forecasts (ECMWF) Inte<sub>g</sub>rated Forecastin<sub>g</sub> S<sub>y</sub>stem (IFS) ensemble (IFS-ENS) is the canonical exam<sub>p</sub>le, with 50 members <sub>p</sub>erturbed b<sub>y</sub> an Ensemble of Data Assimilations (EDA), sin<sub>g</sub>ular vectors, and stochastic model <sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>sics (Buizza et al. 1999; Leutbecher et al. 2017), but runnin<sub>g</sub> it is com<sub>p</sub>utationall<sub>y</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>ensive (Bauer et al. 2015; Bauer 2024). In <sub>p</sub>arallel, deterministic machine-learnin<sub>g</sub> weather models (MLWMs) (e.<sub>g</sub>., Pathak et al. 2022; Bi et al. 2023; Lam et al. 2023; Lan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024a; Bodnar et al. 2025; Bonev et al. 2023) now match or beat the IFS at 0.25<sup>◦</sup> resolution (Boualle<sub>g</sub>ue et al. 2024) at far lower inference cost, but are deterministic b<sub>y</sub> construction, <sub>so</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t <sub>carr</sub>i<sub>es</sub> <sub>no</sub> <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>uncer</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>y.</sub> Wh<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>cap</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>a</sub>t<sub>mosp</sub>h<sub>er</sub>i<sub>c</sub> d<sub>y</sub>namics rather than statistical re<sub>g</sub>ularities of the trainin<sub>g</sub> record remains contested (e.<sub>g</sub>., Smith and Thor<sub>p</sub>e 2026), makin<sub>g</sub> calibrated uncertaint<sub>y</sub> central to their o<sub>p</sub>erational use.

Four remedies have emerged, which we review in sec. 2. Trained-probabilistic (TP) MLWMs l<sub>earn</sub> t<sub>o genera</sub>t<sub>e an ensem</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y,</sub> t<sub>yp</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>a con</sub>ti<sub>nuous ran</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y score</sub> (CRPS) or difusion objective (e.<sub>g</sub>., Price et al. 2023; Lan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024b; Bonev et al. 2025; Kossaifi et al. 2026; Alet et al. 2025); the<sub>y</sub> are calibrated b<sub>y</sub> desi<sub>g</sub>n but carr<sub>y</sub> the cost of a dedicated probabilistic training run (sec. 4.d). Initial-condition perturbation rolls out a deterministic network from <sub>p</sub>erturbed anal<sub>y</sub>ses (Selz and Crai<sub>g</sub> 2023): such <sub>p</sub>erturbations are chea<sub>p</sub> but s<sub>p</sub>read-limited because a deterministic network ma<sub>p</sub>s nearb<sub>y</sub> in<sub>p</sub>uts to nearl<sub>y</sub> identical out<sub>p</sub>uts (B¨ulte et al. 2024). Post-hoc perturbation generates members by perturbing the weights or activations of one pretrained deterministic check<sub>p</sub>oint durin<sub>g</sub> inference at no extra trainin<sub>g</sub> cost (Scher and Messori 2021; Graubner et al. 2022; Hu et al. 2022). Finally, a deep ensemble of independently trained check<sub>p</sub>oints (Mahesh et al. 2024) <sub>p</sub>a<sub>y</sub>s a full trainin<sub>g</sub> run <sub>p</sub>er check<sub>p</sub>oint; we include it onl<sub>y</sub> as a <sub>concep</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>-</sub>h<sub>oc</sub> <sub>rou</sub>t<sub>e.</sub>

Of the four, <sub>p</sub>ost-hoc <sub>p</sub>erturbation alone is trainin<sub>g</sub>-free (unlike trained-<sub>p</sub>robabilistic models and dee<sub>p</sub> ensembles) <sub>y</sub>et <sub>y</sub>ields s<sub>p</sub>read that <sub>g</sub>rows with lead time (unlike initial-condition <sub>p</sub>erturbation, which we kee<sub>p</sub> onl<sub>y</sub> as a com<sub>p</sub>lementar<sub>y</sub> short-lead time lever, sec. 3.b.3). A network has no <sub>p</sub>arametrisations to <sub>p</sub>erturb, but it does have weights, so we perturb those and call the scheme stochastically perturbed weights (SPW). Three questions organise the paper. Q1: does simple weight perturbation work at all, and how far behind purpose-built probabilistic models does it leave the ensemble? Q2: where in the network, and on which spatial scales, should the noise be injected? Q3: where does the scheme fail, and how much can refreshin<sub>g</sub> the noise across the autore<sub>g</sub>ressive (AR) rollout and <sub>p</sub>erturbin<sub>g</sub> the initial conditions repair? Secs. 4.a–4.b answer Q1 and Q2 and sec. 4.c answers Q3; the Milton case stud<sub>y</sub>, the cost <sub>accoun</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>appen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> di<sub>agnos</sub>ti<sub>cs</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>suppor</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence.</sub>

## 2. Background and Related Work

## a. Deterministic ML weather models

Most MLWMs share a common reci<sub>p</sub>e: train on 40+ <sub>y</sub>ears of ERA5 (Hersbach et al. 2020) t<sub>o map</sub> th<sub>e g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l <sub>a</sub>t<sub>mosp</sub>h<sub>er</sub>i<sub>c s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> 6 h<sub>,</sub> th<sub>en au</sub>t<sub>oregress</sub> t<sub>o a mu</sub>lti<sub>-</sub>d<sub>ay</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>ey</sub> dif<sub>er</sub> <sub>ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure,</sub> i<sub>.e.,</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> bi<sub>as.</sub> S<sub>p</sub>h<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d <sub>spec</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l <sub>opera</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> re<sub>p</sub>lace <sub>p</sub>lanar convolutions with s<sub>p</sub>herical-harmonic filters (FourCastNet (Pathak et al. 2022), the S<sub>p</sub>herical Fourier Neural O<sub>p</sub>erator (SFNO) (Bonev et al. 2023)); <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h neural networks (GNNs) encode the <sub>g</sub>rid onto a refined icosahedral mesh and <sub>p</sub>ass messa<sub>g</sub>es on it (Gra<sub>p</sub>hCast (Lam et al. 2023), the Artificial Intelli<sub>g</sub>ence Forecastin<sub>g</sub> S<sub>y</sub>stem (AIFS) (Lan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024a)). Transformer and o<sub>p</sub>erator-learnin<sub>g</sub> backbones are also widel<sub>y</sub> used: Pan<sub>g</sub>u-Weather (Bi et al. 2023), Aurora (Bodnar et al. 2025), ESFM (Ozdemir et al. 2026), ClimaX (N<sub>g</sub>u<sub>y</sub>en et al. 2023a), Stormer (N<sub>g</sub>u<sub>y</sub>en et al. 2023b), FuXi (Chen et al. 2023), and the h<sub>y</sub>brid diferentiable-solver NeuralGCM (Kochkov et al. 2024). ## b. Probabilistic ML weather forecasting

M<sub>os</sub>t <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bili<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> MLWM<sub>s are</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d t<sub>o pro</sub>d<sub>uce an ensem</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y,</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>ti<sub>ng ca</sub>lib<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f the full joint predictive distribution rather than the per-gridpoint marginals alone. CRPS-loss models o<sub>p</sub>timise an (almost-)fair CRPS: AIFS-ENS (Lan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024b) injects an inde<sub>p</sub>endent Gaussian noise field on its O96 latent (<sub>p</sub>rocessor) <sub>g</sub>rid (40,320 nodes) <sub>p</sub>er member and time ste<sub>p</sub>, throu<sub>g</sub>h the conditional la<sub>y</sub>er normalisation of ever<sub>y p</sub>rocessor block, and FourCastNet 3 (Bonev et al. 2025) pairs a spatially and spectrally weighted CRPS objective with per-step spherical-difusion noise. Sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub>-based models draw members from learned noise: GenCast (Price et al. 2023) denoises a s<sub>p</sub>here-aware Gaussian sam<sub>p</sub>le, Atlas-ERA5 (Kossaifi et al. 2026) inte<sub>g</sub>rates a stochastic diferential equation (SDE) (Alber<sub>g</sub>o and Vanden-Eijnden 2023) in a latent s<sub>p</sub>ace, and Oskarsson et al. (2024) use a hierarchical latent-variable mesh-<sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h. The Functional Generative Network (Alet et al. 2025) sits between the families, broadcastin<sub>g</sub> one <sub>g</sub>lobal noise vector <sub>p</sub>er member throu<sub>g</sub>h <sub>every con</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l l<sub>ayer-norm, a</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er-space ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on;</sub> U<sub>-</sub>C<sub>as</sub>t (Cacha<sub>y</sub> et al. 2026) matches GenCast and IFS-ENS at 1.5<sup>◦</sup> with a <sub>p</sub>lain CRPS-tuned U-Net; and SEEDS (Li et al. 2024) emulates an existin<sub>g</sub> NWP ensemble rather than the atmos<sub>p</sub>here. We b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t AIFS<sub>-</sub>ENS<sub>,</sub> FCN3 <sub>an</sub>d Atl<sub>as, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>span</sub> th<sub>ese arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ures, are w</sub>id<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y use</sub>d<sub>,</sub> and each <sub>p</sub>a<sub>y</sub> a dedicated <sub>p</sub>robabilistic trainin<sub>g</sub> cost (sec. 4.d).

A di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>nc</sub>t f<sub>am</sub>il<sub>y pro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> th<sub>e w</sub>h<sub>o</sub>l<sub>e ensem</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rom a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>one pass ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an one</sub> rollout <sub>p</sub>er member, as <sub>p</sub>er-<sub>g</sub>rid<sub>p</sub>oint distribution <sub>p</sub>arameters (Gar<sub>g</sub> et al. 2022) or li<sub>g</sub>htwei<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>p</sub>er-member heads on a shared latent s<sub>p</sub>ace (Lessi<sub>g</sub> et al. 2023; WeatherGenerator Consortium 2025; Ozdemir et al. 2026). Bound to one deterministic latent trajectory, it cannot capture the trajectory divergence an autoregressive perturbation produces, trading bespoke training cost for <sub>c</sub>h<sub>eap</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>m</sub>i<sub>rror</sub> i<sub>mage</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>-</sub>h<sub>oc</sub> <sub>rou</sub>t<sub>e.</sub>

## 1) The three-phase ablation

## 2) Refreshing the perturbation across the rollout

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>a</sub>bl<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>reezes</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>across</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>:</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>mem</sub>b<sub>er</sub> d<sub>raws</sub> $\xi _ { m }$ <sub>once</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>reuses</sub> it f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e w</sub>h<sub>o</sub>l<sub>e ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t $( T = 4 0 \mathrm { A R }$ ste<sub>p</sub>s at the ablation <sub>g</sub>rid’s 240 h), so the <sub>p</sub>erturbation <sub>p</sub>ushes the <sub>spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>mean</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>every</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>per-mem</sub>b<sub>er</sub> <sub>o</sub>f<sub>se</sub>t <sub>accumu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>co</sub>h<sub>eren</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> with lead time (sec. 4.c). The refresh-ever<sub>y</sub>-�-ste<sub>p</sub>s variant instead holds the noise fixed for � <sub>consecu</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>eps</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>resamp</sub>l<sub>es</sub> it <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>nex</sub>t b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary,</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> <sub>var</sub>i<sub>ance-</sub>b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t <sub>ru</sub>l<sub>e</sub>

$$
\sigma _ { N } = \sigma _ { \mathrm { f r o z e n } } \sqrt { T / N }\tag{2}
$$

(i) Which configuration is used where. Three arms per backbone run on the full 112-init grid. Weight-only is the production baseline and carries every reported result unless stated otherwise. Weight+IC (sufix ic) adds the perturbed analyses and is used only for the Milton case study (sec. 4.e). IC-only (sufix ic only) carries no wei<sub>g</sub>ht noise and serves solel<sub>y</sub> as the control for th<sub>e a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n sec.</sub> 4<sub>.c.</sub>

## 5. Discussion

## a. Architecture-specific tuning, not a plug-and-play recipe

Q1: does simple weight perturbation work, and how far behind purpose-built probabilistic models does it leave the ensemble? It works. A three-phase framework (magnitude, tensor <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>, coarse scale) turns four deterministic MLWMs into ensembles that trail the best trained baseline (AIFS-ENS) b<sub>y</sub> 0.04 (<sub>p</sub>erturbed AIFS) to 0.13 CRPSS at 240 h on a 112-init <sub>g</sub>rid, at zero <sub>marg</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>cos</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d 59<sub>–</sub>125 <sub>secon</sub>d<sub>s</sub> <sub>per</sub> <sub>mem</sub>b<sub>er,</sub> 18<sub>–</sub>39 ti<sub>mes</sub> f<sub>as</sub>t<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> th<sub>e</sub> SDE<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d Atl<sub>as.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>gaps</sub> <sub>narrow</sub> b<sub>y</sub> 360 h <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>every</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>converges</sub> t<sub>owar</sub>d <sub>c</sub>li<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>ogy.</sub>

Q3: where does the scheme fail, and what do refreshing the noise across the rollout and perturbing the initial conditions repair? It fails on the domain mean, and the repairs are partial.
